\Pi_{\leq q^{\prime}}P\circ\Phi^{t}_{\chi}=\Pi_{\leq q^{\prime}}P.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
\Pi_{0}P=0
. Indeed, since
\chi
commutes with the
L^{2}
norm, we have
(\Pi_{0}P)\circ\Phi^{t}_{\chi}=\Pi_{0}P
.

Then, we want to apply the Cauchy–Kowaleskaya lemma proven in the appendix (see Lemma
10.1
). So we set, for
p\geq 1
,

E^{(p)}=\Pi_{p}\mathcal{P}_{\eta_{0},r}
and
\|\cdot\|_{E^{(p)}}:=\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{P}_{\eta_{0},r}}
.
We note that, with the notations of Lemma
10.1
, we have for any
\beta\geq 0

E_{\beta}=(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{0})\mathscr{P}_{\eta_{0}+\beta,r}\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\|\cdot\|_{\beta}=\|\cdot\|_{\mathscr{P}_{\eta_{0}+\beta,r}}.

By Lemma
2.15
, we have for all
0\leq\beta\leq\eta-\eta_{0}
, all
p\geq 1
, all
P\in E_{\beta}

\|\Pi_{p}\{P,\chi\}\|_{E^{(p)}}\lesssim\ pe^{-\beta p}\|P\|_{\beta}r^{-2}(q^{\prime})^{5}\|\chi\|_{\mathscr{P}_{\eta,r}}

and thus the linear map
L:=\{\cdot,\chi\}
satisfies (
173
) with
C_{L}\sim r^{-2}(q^{\prime})^{5}\|\chi\|_{\mathscr{P}_{\eta,r}}
. As a consequence of (
29
) we have
2C_{L}\leq\alpha
.

Now let
P\in\mathscr{P}_{\eta,r}
. Applying Lemma
10.1
, there exists
t\mapsto P_{t}\in C^{1}([0,1];\mathscr{P}_{\eta-\alpha,r})
solution of the transport equation

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\partial_{t}P_{t}=\{P_{t},\chi\},\quad t\in[0,1]\\
P_{0}=P.\end{array}\right.\quad\mathrm{such\ that}\quad\sup_{0\leq t\leq 1}\quad\|P_{t}\|_{\mathscr{P}_{\eta-\alpha,r}}\leq 2\|P\|_{\mathscr{P}_{\eta,r}}.

Now consider
g(t)=P_{t}\circ\Phi_{\chi}^{-t}(u)
for some
u\in B_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}(0,2r)
. We have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}g(t)

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}P_{t}\circ\Phi_{\chi}^{-t}(u)+\mathrm{d}P_{t}(\Phi_{\chi}^{-t}(u))(\partial_{t}\Phi_{\chi}^{-t}(u))

\displaystyle=\{P_{t},\chi\}\circ\Phi_{\chi}^{-t}(u)-(\mathrm{i}\nabla P_{t}(\Phi_{\chi}^{-t}(u)),\nabla\chi(\Phi_{\chi}^{-t}(u)))_{\ell^{2}}=0

and thus
P_{t}\circ\Phi_{\chi}^{-t}(u)=g(t)=g(0)=P(u)
. Therefore
P_{t}=P\circ\Phi_{\chi}^{t}
and we deduce that
P\circ\Phi_{\chi}^{t}
belongs to
\mathscr{P}_{\eta-\alpha,r}
for all
t\in[0,1]
and satisfies (
30
).

Finally it remains to prove (
31
). First, we note that by Lemma
2.15
and preservation of the mass
\Pi_{\leq q^{\prime}-1}\{P_{t},\chi\}=0
. Since
q^{\prime}\geq 4
, it follows that
\partial_{t}\Pi_{\leq 2}P_{t}=\Pi_{\leq 2}\{P_{t},\chi\}=0
. Since
\Pi_{2}P=0
(and
\Pi_{1}P=0
by preservation of the mass) it follows that
\Pi_{\leq 2}P_{t}=\Pi_{\leq 2}P=\Pi_{0}P
. Therefore, applying again Lemma
2.15
and using the preservation of the mass, we get
\Pi_{\leq q^{\prime}-1}\{P_{t},\chi\}=0
and it follows that
\partial_{t}\Pi_{\leq q^{\prime}}P_{t}=\Pi_{\leq q^{\prime}}\{P_{t},\chi\}=0
. Thus, we have that
\Pi_{\leq q^{\prime}}P\circ\Phi_{\chi}^{t}\equiv\Pi_{\leq q^{\prime}}P_{t}=\Pi_{\leq q^{\prime}}P
.

∎

2.5.
Terms in normal form and cohomological equations

In this subsection, we first define a notion of normal form and introduce a notation for the quadratic part of the Hamiltonian of (
NLS
). Then we justify it by two useful lemmas.

Definition 2.18

(
Z_{2}
)
.

We define the quadratic part of the Hamiltonian of (
NLS
) by

\forall u\in\ell^{2}_{\varpi},\quad Z_{2}(u)=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}k^{2}|u_{k}|^{2}.

Definition 2.19

(Terms in normal form)
.

Let
r\in(0,1)
and
\eta\geq 0
.
A Hamiltonian
P\in\mathscr{P}_{\eta,r}
is in normal form if its non zero coefficients
P_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\neq 0
satisfy

(32)

(\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma})\in\mathbf{P}_{q}\quad\mathrm{or}\quad\big|\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{1}\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}^{2}+\cdots+\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{q}\boldsymbol{\ell}_{q}^{2}\big|\leq 4^{-10}q^{9}.

We denote by
\Pi_{\mathrm{nf}}
the associated projection defined by restriction of the coefficients.

Then, we prove that the terms in normal form are actually smoothing (up to a gauge transform).

Lemma 2.20

(Terms in normal form are smoothing)
.

For all
r\in(0,1)
and
\eta\geq\eta_{0}
, we have that

\Pi_{\mathrm{nf}}\mathscr{P}_{\eta,r}\subset\mathscr{R}_{\eta,r}.

Proof.

We have to prove that if
q\geq 0
,
\boldsymbol{\ell}\in\mathbb{Z}^{q}
and
\boldsymbol{\sigma}\in\{-1,1\}^{q}
are such that,
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{1}\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}+\cdots+\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{q}\boldsymbol{\ell}_{q}=0
,
(\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma})\notin\mathbf{P}_{q}
and
\big|\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{1}\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}^{2}+\cdots+\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{q}\boldsymbol{\ell}_{q}^{2}\big|\leq 4^{-10}q^{9}
then
\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}^{*}\rangle\lesssim e^{q}\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}_{3}^{*}\rangle^{2}
.

The cases
q\leq 2
are implied by the case
q=3
. So we assume from now that
q\geq 3
and, moreover, without loss of generality, that
|\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}|\geq\cdots\geq|\boldsymbol{\ell}_{q}|
. We distinguish two cases.

—

Case
(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{1})=-(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{2})
.
Using the zero momentum condition, we have that

2|\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}|=|\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{3}\boldsymbol{\ell}_{3}+\cdots+\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{q}\boldsymbol{\ell}_{q}|\leq(q-2)|\boldsymbol{\ell}_{3}|.

—

Case
(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{1})\neq-(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{2})
.
Since by assumption, we also have that
(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{1})\neq(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2},-\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{2})
, we deduce that

|\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}|\leq|\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{1}\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}^{2}+\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{2}\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2}^{2}|\leq 4^{-10}q^{9}+(q-2)\boldsymbol{\ell}_{3}^{2}.

We conclude by noticing that both estimates imply that
\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}\rangle\lesssim e^{q}\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}_{3}\rangle^{2}
.

∎

Finally, we prove that the solutions of the cohomological equations are also smoothing (up to a gauge transform).

Lemma 2.21

(Solutions to the cohomological equations are smoothing)
.

For all
r\in(0,1)
,
\eta\geq\eta_{0}
,
q\geq 3
and
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\eta,r}

there exists
\chi\in\Pi_{q}\mathcal{R}_{\eta,r}
such that

(33)

\forall u\in B_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}(0,3r),\quad\{Z_{2},\chi\}(u)=-\Pi_{q}(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\mathrm{nf}})P(u)

and

(34)

\|\chi\|_{\mathscr{R}_{\eta,r}}\lesssim q^{-9}\|P\|_{\mathscr{P}_{\eta,r}}.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
P=\Pi_{q}(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\mathrm{nf}})P
. First, we define a Hamiltonian
\chi\in\Pi_{q}\mathcal{P}_{\eta,r}
by setting

\chi_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}=0
if
P_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}=0
and, else

\chi_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}:=\frac{P_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}{\mathrm{i}\Omega_{\boldsymbol{\ell,\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}}\quad\mathrm{where}\quad\Omega_{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}:=\sum_{1\leq j\leq q}\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{j}\boldsymbol{\ell}_{j}^{2}.

We note that it is clear that
\chi\in\Pi_{q}\mathcal{P}_{\eta,r}
and a standard direct calculation proves that
\chi
solves the comohological equation (
33
). The main point is to prove that
\chi\in\Pi_{q}\mathcal{R}_{\eta,r}
and satisfies the bound (
34
).

By definition, to prove it, it suffices to prove that if
(\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma})\in\mathbb{Z}^{q}\times\{-1,1\}^{q}
are such that
(\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma})\notin\mathbf{P}_{q}
,
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{1}\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}+\cdots+\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{q}\boldsymbol{\ell}_{q}=0
and
|\Omega_{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}|>4^{-10}q^{9}
then

|\chi_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}|\lesssim q^{-9}|P_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}|\quad\mathrm{and}\quad e^{-q}\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}^{*}\rangle}{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}_{3}^{*}\rangle^{2}}|\chi_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}|\lesssim q^{-9}|P_{n}^{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}|.

The first estimate is a direct consequence of the lower bound on
|\Omega_{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}|
. For the second one, we assume moreover, without loss of generality that
|\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}|\geq\cdots\geq|\boldsymbol{\ell}_{q}|
and we aim at proving that

(35)

\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}\rangle}{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}_{3}\rangle^{2}}|\Omega_{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}|^{-1}\lesssim e^{q/2}.

We have to distinguish
2
cases.

—

Case
(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{1})=-(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{2})
.
Using the zero momentum condition, we have that

(36)

2|\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}|=|\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{3}\boldsymbol{\ell}_{3}+\cdots+\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{q}\boldsymbol{\ell}_{q}|\leq(q-2)|\boldsymbol{\ell}_{3}|.

—

Case
(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{1})\neq-(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{2})
.
Since by assumption, we also have that
(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{1})\neq(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2},-\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{2})
, we deduce that

|\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}|\leq|\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{1}\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}^{2}+\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{2}\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2}^{2}|\leq|\Omega_{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}|+(q-2)\boldsymbol{\ell}_{3}^{2}.

As a consequence, we have

(37)

\mathrm{either}\quad|\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}|\leq(q-2)\boldsymbol{\ell}_{3}^{2}\quad\mathrm{or}\quad\frac{1}{2}|\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}|\leq|\Omega_{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}|.

Finally, recalling that, by assumption, we also have
|\Omega_{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}|\geq 1
, it suffices to conclude to note that both (
36
) and (
37
) imply (
35
).
∎

2.6.
Iterative lemma

In this subsection, we prove a lemma which is somehow the heredity step of our regularizing normal form procedure.

Lemma 2.22

(Iterative lemma)
.

Let
r\in(0,1)
,
q\geq 4
,
\eta^{\prime}=\eta-q^{-2}\geq\eta_{0}
and
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\eta,r}
be such that

\Pi_{\leq q-1}P=\Pi_{\mathrm{nf}}\Pi_{\leq q-1}P,\quad\Pi_{2}P=0\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\|P\|_{\mathcal{P}_{\eta,r}}\lesssim r^{2}.

Then, there exists
\chi\in\Pi_{q}\mathcal{R}_{\eta,r}
and
Q\in\mathcal{P}_{\eta^{\prime},r}
such that

—

Q
is in normal form up to order
q
, i.e.

\Pi_{\leq q}Q=\Pi_{\mathrm{nf}}\Pi_{\leq q}P,

—

Z_{2}+P
is conjugated to
Z_{2}+Q
, i.e.

(Z_{2}+P)\circ\Phi_{\chi}^{1}=Z_{2}+Q\quad\mathrm{on}\quad B_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}(0,2r)

—

Q
and
\chi
satisfy the bounds

\|\chi\|_{\mathcal{R}_{\eta,r}}\lesssim q^{-9}\|P\|_{\mathcal{P}_{\eta,r}}\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\|Q\|_{\mathcal{P}_{\eta^{\prime},r}}\leq(1+q^{-2})\|P\|_{\mathcal{P}_{\eta,r}}.

Proof.

Let
\chi
be the Hamiltonian given by Lemma
2.21
. We note that
\|\chi\|_{\mathcal{P}_{\eta,r}}\lesssim\|P\|_{\mathcal{P}_{\eta,r}}
and so, provided that
r^{-2}\|P\|_{\mathcal{P}_{\eta,r}}
is small enough,
\Phi_{\chi}^{t}
is well defined up to time
1
by Lemma
2.13
.

Then, by Taylor expansion, we have on
B_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}(0,2r)
that

(Z_{2}+P)\circ\Phi_{\chi}^{1}=Z_{2}+K+R^{(1)}+R^{(2)}=:Z_{2}+Q

where
K=\{Z_{2},\chi\}+P
,

R^{(1)}=\int_{0}^{1}\{P,\chi\}\circ\Phi_{\chi}^{t}\mathrm{d}t\quad\mathrm{and}\quad R^{(2)}=\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)\{\{Z_{2},\chi\},\chi\}\circ\Phi_{\chi}^{t}\mathrm{d}t.

Then, we recall that by construction of
\chi
, we have
\{Z_{2},\chi\}=-\Pi_{q}(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\mathrm{nf}})P\in\mathcal{P}_{\eta,r}
. Therefore we deduce that
K\in\mathcal{P}_{\eta,r}
satisfies

\|K\|_{\mathcal{P}_{\eta,r}}\leq\|P\|_{\mathcal{P}_{\eta,r}}\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\Pi_{\leq q}K=\Pi_{\mathrm{nf}}\Pi_{\leq q}P.

Now, we focus on
R^{(1)}
. Since
\|\chi\|_{\mathscr{R}_{\eta,r}}\lesssim q^{-9}\|P\|_{\mathscr{P}_{\eta,r}}
, provided that
r^{-2}\|P\|_{\mathscr{P}_{\eta,r}}
is small enough, we can apply Lemma
2.17
with
\alpha=q^{-2}
, to deduce that
R^{(1)}\in\mathscr{P}_{\eta^{\prime},r}
and

\|R^{(1)}\|_{\mathscr{P}_{\eta^{\prime},r}}\lesssim\|\{\chi,P\}\|_{\mathscr{P}_{\eta^{\prime},r}}.

Then applying the Poisson bracket estimate of Corollary
2.16
, we deduce that

\|R^{(1)}\|_{\mathscr{P}_{\eta^{\prime},r}}\lesssim r^{-2}(\eta-\eta^{\prime})^{-1}q^{5}\|\chi\|_{\mathscr{R}_{\eta,r}}\|P\|_{\mathscr{P}_{\eta,r}}\lesssim q^{-2}\big(r^{-2}\|P\|_{\mathscr{P}_{\eta,r}}\big)\|P\|_{\mathscr{P}_{\eta,r}}

and so, provided that
r^{-2}\|P\|_{\mathscr{P}_{\eta,r}}
is small enough that
\|R^{(1)}\|_{\mathscr{P}_{\eta^{\prime},r}}\leq\frac{1}{2}q^{-2}\|P\|_{\mathscr{P}_{\eta,r}}
.
Using that
\{Z_{2},\chi\}=-\Pi_{q}(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\mathrm{nf}})P\in\mathcal{P}_{\eta,r}
, we deduce similarly that
R^{(2)}\in\mathscr{P}_{\eta^{\prime},r}
and that it also satisfies
\|R^{(2)}\|_{\mathscr{P}_{\eta^{\prime},r}}\leq\frac{1}{2}q^{-2}\|P\|_{\mathscr{P}_{\eta,r}}
.

Finally, to conclude the proof it suffices to prove that
\Pi_{\leq q}R^{(1)}=\Pi_{\leq q}R^{(2)}=0
.
By Lemma
2.15
, since
\Pi_{2}P=0
, we have that
\Pi_{\leq 2}P=\Pi_{0}P
and so by preservation of the mass that
\Pi_{\leq q}\{P,\chi\}=\Pi_{\leq q}\{\Pi_{0}P,\chi\}=0
. Therefore since
q\geq 4
, using the relation (
31
) of Lemma
2.17
, we have that
\Pi_{\leq q}\{P,\chi\}\circ\Phi_{\chi}^{t}=0
(for
t\in[0,1]
) and so that
\Pi_{\leq q}R^{(1)}=0
. The same arguments apply to prove that
\Pi_{\leq q}R^{(2)}=0
.

∎

2.7.
Convergence of the regularizing normal form : proof of Theorem
2.1

We divide the proof in
4
steps.

Step
1
: Setting.

First, we recall that by Proposition
2.9
, there exist
r_{0}\in(0,1)
and
P^{\eqref{eq:NLS}}\in\mathcal{P}_{3\eta_{0},r_{0}}
such that

H^{\eqref{eq:NLS}}=Z_{2}+P^{\eqref{eq:NLS}}\quad\mathrm{on}\quad B_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}(0,3r_{0}),

\Pi_{\mathrm{deg}\leq 3}P^{\eqref{eq:NLS}}=0
and
\Pi_{2}P^{\eqref{eq:NLS}}=0
. Therefore, we have by homogeneity that for all
r\leq r_{0}
,

\forall r\leq r_{0},\quad\|P^{\eqref{eq:NLS}}\|_{\mathcal{P}(3\eta_{0},r)}\lesssim r^{4}.

From now, we fix a radius
r\leq r_{0}
such that
\|P^{\eqref{eq:NLS}}\|_{\mathcal{P}(3\eta_{0},r)}\leq r^{3}
and that we assume small enough (i.e. in other words, we will assume a finite number of extra smallness assumptions on
r
).

We set

\eta_{q}=3\eta_{0}-\sum_{4\leq p<q}p^{-2},\quad\mathrm{for}\quad q\geq 4.

Applying the iterative lemma, provided that
r
is small enough, we get some families of Hamiltonians
\chi^{(q)}\in\Pi_{q}\mathcal{R}_{\eta_{q},r}
and
P^{(q)}\in\mathcal{P}_{\eta_{q},r}
such that for all
q\geq 4

—

P^{(4)}=P^{\eqref{eq:NLS}}
,

—

P^{(q+1)}
is in normal form up to order
q
and stationary in some sense, i.e.

(38)

\Pi_{\leq q}P^{(q+1)}=\Pi_{\mathrm{nf}}\Pi_{\leq q}P^{(q)},

—

Z_{2}+P^{(q)}
is conjugated to
Z_{2}+P^{(q+1)}
, i.e.

(39)

(Z_{2}+P^{(q)})\circ\Phi_{\chi^{(q)}}^{1}=Z_{2}+P^{(q+1)}\quad\mathrm{on}\quad B_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}(0,2r)

—

they enjoy the bounds

(40)

\|\chi^{(q)}\|_{\mathcal{P}_{\eta_{q},r}}\lesssim q^{-9}r^{3}\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\|P^{(q)}\|_{\mathcal{P}_{\eta_{q},r}}\leq C_{q}r^{3}\quad\mathrm{where}\quad C_{q}:=\prod_{4\leq p<q}1+p^{-2}.

Thanks to the bound (
40
) on
\chi^{(q)}
, we know, by Lemma
2.13
that, provided that
r
is small enough, for all
q\geq 4
and
\lambda\in(1,2)
,
\Phi_{\chi^{(q)}}^{1}
maps
B_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}(0,\lambda r)
into
B_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}(0,(1+\varepsilon q^{-2})\lambda r)
where
\varepsilon>0
is a constant so that

\prod_{q\geq 4}1+\varepsilon q^{-2}\leq 2.

As a consequence, for
q\geq 4
, the map

\tau^{(q)}:=\Phi_{\chi^{(4)}}^{1}\circ\cdots\circ\Phi_{\chi^{(q-1)}}^{1}:B_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}(0,r)\to B_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}(0,3r)\quad\mathrm{is\ well\ defined}.

Moreover, for all
q\geq 4
,
\tau^{(q)}
is clearly smooth, symplectic and satisfies, by (
39
),

(41)

(Z_{2}+P^{\eqref{eq:NLS}})\circ\tau^{(q)}=Z_{2}+P^{(q)}\quad\mathrm{on}\quad B_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}(0,r).

Step
2
: Convergence of
P^{(q)}
.
We aim at passing to the limit as
q
goes to
+\infty
. First, thanks to the relation (
38
), it make sense to define
P^{(\infty)}\in\mathcal{P}_{\eta_{\infty},r}
by

\forall q\geq 4,\quad\Pi_{\leq q}P^{(\infty)}:=\Pi_{\mathrm{nf}}\Pi_{\leq q}P^{(q)}

and by the estimate (
40
), it satisfies
\|P^{(\infty)}\|_{\mathcal{P}_{\eta_{\infty},r}}\leq C_{\infty}r^{3}
. Moreover, we note that by (
38
),
P^{(\infty)}
is in normal form, i.e.

P^{(\infty)}=\Pi_{\mathrm{nr}}P^{(\infty)}.

Then, we aim at proving that
P^{(q)}
converges pointwize to
P^{(\infty)}
on
B_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}(0,r)
. So let
u\in B_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}(0,r)
,
q\geq 4
and set
\rho:=\|u\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}<r
. First, we note that by construction,

\Pi_{\leq q-1}P^{(q)}=\Pi_{\leq q-1}P^{(\infty)}.

Therefore, we have by homogeneity that

\|P^{(q)}-P^{(\infty)}\|_{\mathcal{P}_{\eta_{\infty},\rho}}\leq\big(\frac{\rho}{r}\big)^{q}\|P^{(q)}-P^{(\infty)}\|_{\mathcal{P}_{\eta_{\infty},r}}\lesssim r^{3}\big(\frac{\rho}{r}\big)^{q}.

Thus, applying Lemma
2.6
, we get that

|P^{(q)}(u)-P^{(\infty)}(u)|\lesssim\|P^{(q)}-P^{(\infty)}\|_{\mathcal{P}_{\eta_{\infty},\rho}}\lesssim r^{3}\big(\frac{\rho}{r}\big)^{q}\mathop{\longrightarrow}_{q\to\infty}0.

Step
3
: Convergence of
\tau^{(q)}
.
We aim at proving that
\tau^{(q)}
converges in
C^{1}_{b}(B_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}(0,r);\ell^{2}_{\varpi})
to a map
\tau^{(\infty)}
. We are going to prove that it is a Cauchy sequence. So let
4\leq p\leq q
and
u\in B_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}(0,r)
. Applying the triangular inequality and the mean value inequality, we get that

\begin{split}\|\tau^{(q)}(u)-\tau^{(p)}(u)\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}&\leq\sum_{j=p+1}^{q}\|\tau^{(j)}(u)-\tau^{(j-1)}(u)\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\\
&\leq\sum_{j=p+1}^{q}\sup_{v\in B_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}(0,(1+\varepsilon j^{-2})r)}\|\mathrm{d}\tau^{(j-1)}(v)\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}\to\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\|u-\Phi_{\chi^{(j)}}^{1}(u)\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\end{split}

Then, we note that, by Lemma
2.13
we have

\|\mathrm{d}\tau^{(j-1)}(v)\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}\to\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\leq\prod_{4\leq\ell\leq j-2}1+r^{-2}\|\chi^{(\ell)}\|_{\mathcal{P}_{\eta_{q},r}}\mathop{\lesssim}^{\eqref{eq:bound_induction}}1.

Therefore, using (
40
) and the bound given by Corollary
2.14
, we have that

(42)

\|\tau^{(q)}(u)-\tau^{(p)}(u)\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\lesssim r^{-1}\|u\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}^{3}\sum_{j=p+1}^{+\infty}j^{-9}.

Similarly, still using the bound given by Lemma
2.13
and (
40
), we get that

(43)

\begin{split}\|\mathrm{d}\tau^{(q)}(u)-\mathrm{d}\tau^{(p)}(u)\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}\to\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\lesssim\sum_{j=p+1}^{q}\|\mathrm{Id}-\mathrm{d}\Phi_{\chi^{(j)}}^{1}(u)\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}\to\to\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\lesssim r\sum_{j=p+1}^{q}j^{-9}.\end{split}

The estimates (
42
) and (
43
) prove that
(\tau^{(q)})_{q}
is a Cauchy sequence in
C^{1}_{b}(B_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}(0,r);\ell^{2}_{\varpi})
which is a Banach space. Thus it converges in this space and we denote by
\tau^{(\infty)}\in C^{1}_{b}(B_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}(0,r);\ell^{2}_{\varpi})
its limit.

Step
4
: Conclusion.
Passing to the limit in (
41
), we have that

(Z_{2}+P^{\eqref{eq:NLS}})\circ\tau^{(\infty)}=Z_{2}+P^{(\infty)}\quad\mathrm{on}\quad B_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}(0,r).

Since the maps
\tau^{(q)}
preserve the
L^{2}
norm, are symplectic and are invariant by gauge transform, the same is true for
\tau^{(\infty)}
. Moreover passing to the limit as
q\to\infty
in (
42
) and (
43
) for
p=4
, we have that (provided that
r
is small enough)

\|\tau^{(\infty)}(u)-\mathrm{Id}\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\lesssim r^{-1}\|u\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}^{3}\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\|\mathrm{d}\tau^{(\infty)}(u)-\mathrm{Id}\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}\to\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

Since
P^{(\infty)}
is in normal form, we have by Lemma
2.20
that
P^{(\infty)}\in\mathcal{R}_{\eta_{\infty},r}
. Finally, to conclude this proof it suffices to prove that
\Pi_{\mathrm{deg}\leq 5}P^{(\infty)}=H^{(0)}_{1}-Z_{2}
.

First, we note that by conservation of the mass,
\Pi_{\mathrm{deg}\leq 5}P^{(\infty)}=\Pi_{\mathrm{deg}\leq 4}P^{(\infty)}
. Then, we note that by construction

\Pi_{\mathrm{deg}\leq 4}P^{(\infty)}=\Pi_{\mathrm{deg}\leq 4}\Pi_{\leq 4}P^{(\infty)}=\Pi_{\mathrm{deg}\leq 4}\Pi_{\leq 4}P^{(4)}=\Pi_{\mathrm{deg}\leq 4}\Pi_{\mathrm{nf}}\Pi_{\leq 4}P^{\eqref{eq:NLS}}=\Pi_{\mathrm{nf}}\Pi_{\mathrm{deg}\leq 4}P^{\eqref{eq:NLS}}.

Moreover, by uniqueness of the Taylor expansion, we have that for all
v\in\ell^{2}_{\varpi}

\Pi_{\mathrm{deg}\leq 4}P^{\eqref{eq:NLS}}(v)=\frac{f^{\prime}(0)}{4}\|v\|_{L^{4}}^{4}=\frac{f^{\prime}(0)}{4}\sum_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}+\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2}-\boldsymbol{\ell}_{3}-\boldsymbol{\ell}_{4}=0}v_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}}v_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2}}\overline{v_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{3}}}\overline{v_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{4}}}.

Finally, we note that, as in
[
KP96
]
that if
\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}+\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2}-\boldsymbol{\ell}_{3}-\boldsymbol{\ell}_{4}=0
and
|\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}^{2}+\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2}^{2}-\boldsymbol{\ell}_{3}^{2}-\boldsymbol{\ell}_{4}^{2}|\leq\frac{1}{2}
then
(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1},\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2})=(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{3},\boldsymbol{\ell}_{4})
or
(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1},\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2})=(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{4},\boldsymbol{\ell}_{3})
. Thus, we deduce that

\Pi_{\mathrm{deg}\leq 5}P^{(\infty)}(v)=\frac{f^{\prime}(0)}{2}\|v\|_{L^{2}}^{4}-\frac{f^{\prime}(0)}{4}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}|v_{k}|^{2}=(H^{(0)}_{1}-Z_{2})(v).

3.
Analytic Hamiltonians in partial action-angle variables

In this section, we define the framework within which we will present and prove our results and the basic tools that will help us to do so.

3.1.
Spaces of Hamiltonians

Definition 3.1

(Formal series without parameters
\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{cste}}
)
.

Let
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{Z}
be a finite set,
r>0
and
\eta\geq 0
. We denote by
\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{cste}}
, the set of the formal series
P
, of the form

P(\mu,y,z,v):=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathcal{S}}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathcal{S}}}\sum_{q\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{\ell}\in(\mathcal{S}^{c})^{q}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}\in\{-1,1\}^{q}}P_{n,\boldsymbol{k},\boldsymbol{m}}^{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\,\mu^{n}\,\underbrace{\prod_{i=1}^{q}v_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{i}}^{\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{i}}}_{=:v_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}\,\underbrace{\prod_{j\in\mathcal{S}}y^{\boldsymbol{m}_{j}}_{j}z^{\boldsymbol{k}_{j}}_{j}}_{=:y^{\boldsymbol{m}}\,z^{\boldsymbol{k}}}\,

with the usual convention
v_{\ell}^{-1}:=\overline{v_{\ell}}
and whose coefficients
P_{n,\boldsymbol{k},\boldsymbol{m}}^{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\in\mathbb{C}
satisfy the relations

—

reality :
P_{n,-\boldsymbol{k},\boldsymbol{m}}^{\boldsymbol{\ell},-\boldsymbol{\sigma}}=\overline{P_{n,\boldsymbol{k},\boldsymbol{m}}^{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}
,

—

symmetry :
P_{n,\boldsymbol{k},\boldsymbol{m}}^{\varphi\boldsymbol{\ell},\varphi\boldsymbol{\sigma}}=P_{n,\boldsymbol{k},\boldsymbol{m}}^{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}
for all
\varphi\in\mathfrak{S}_{q}
,

—

preservation of the mass : if
P_{n,\boldsymbol{k},\boldsymbol{m}}^{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\neq 0
then

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{S}}\boldsymbol{k}_{j}+\sum_{i=1}^{q}\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{i}=0,

—

preservation of the momentum : if
P_{n,\boldsymbol{k},\boldsymbol{m}}^{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\neq 0
then

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{S}}j\boldsymbol{k}_{j}+\sum_{i=1}^{q}\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{i}\boldsymbol{\ell}_{i}=0

and the bounds

\|P\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{cste}}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{S}}}:=\sup_{n,\boldsymbol{k},\boldsymbol{m},\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}|P_{n,\boldsymbol{k},\boldsymbol{m}}^{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}|e^{\eta(\|\boldsymbol{k}\|_{\ell^{1}}+2\|\boldsymbol{m}\|_{\ell^{1}}+q+2n)}r^{2\|\boldsymbol{m}\|_{\ell^{1}}+q+2n}\Theta_{\ell}^{-1}<\infty

where
15
15

15

by conventions,
\Theta_{\ell}=1
if
q=0
and the second factor equals
(\varpi_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{*}_{1}})^{-1}
when
q=1
.

(44)

\Theta_{\ell}:=1\ \wedge\ \varpi^{\flat}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{*}_{3}}\cdots\varpi^{\flat}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{*}_{q}}\frac{\varpi_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{*}_{2}}}{\varpi_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{*}_{1}}}\ \wedge\ \frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}\rangle^{2\delta}\cdots\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}_{q}\rangle^{2\delta}}{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}^{*}_{1}\rangle^{5\delta}}\ ,

\varpi^{\flat}_{j}:=\langle j\rangle^{-1}\varpi_{j}
and
\boldsymbol{\ell}^{*}_{1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{\ell}^{*}_{q}
is a rearrangement of
\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{\ell}_{q}
satisfying
|\boldsymbol{\ell}^{*}_{1}|\geq\cdots\geq|\boldsymbol{\ell}^{*}_{q}|
.

We will justify this definition, and in particular the choice of the weight
\Theta_{\ell}
, in Remark
3.5
.

Definition 3.2

(Functions
y,\theta,z,\mu
)
.

Being given
u\in L^{2}(\mathbb{T})
,
\xi\in\ell^{1}(\mathbb{Z};\mathbb{R})
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}
we define

—

the re-centered action
y_{k}(\xi;u)=|u_{k}|^{2}-\xi_{k}
,

—

the angle
\theta_{k}(u)\in\mathbb{T}
as an angle satisfying
\sqrt{|u_{k}|}e^{i\theta_{k}(u)}=u_{k}
,

—

z_{k}(u)\in\mathbb{C}
, is the complex number of modulus one defined by
z_{k}(u)=e^{i\theta_{k}}
,

—

the re-centered mass
\mu(\xi;u)=\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-\|\xi\|_{\ell^{1}}
.

Definition 3.3

(Annulus)
.

Being given
\xi\in\ell^{1}_{\varpi^{2}}
and
\rho>0
, we set

\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(\rho)=\Big\{u\in\ell^{2}_{\varpi}\ |\ \sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\big||u_{k}|^{2}-\xi_{k}\big|\varpi_{k}^{2}\,\leq\rho^{2}\Big\}.

Lemma 3.4

(Formal series define functions)
.

Let
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{Z}
be a finite set,
r\in(0,\frac{1}{4})
,
\eta\geq\eta_{0}
,
P\in\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{cste}}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{S}}
and
\xi\in((4r)^{2},1)^{\mathcal{S}}
. Then

u\mapsto P(\mu(\xi;u),y(\xi;u),z(u),u)=:P(\xi;u)

defines a smooth function from
\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(3r)
into
\mathbb{R}
and enjoys the estimate

\sup_{u\in\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(3r)}|P(\xi;u)|\lesssim_{S}\|P\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{cste}}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{S}}}

Proof.

It suffices to prove that, for
\xi\in((4r)^{2},1)^{\mathcal{S}}
, the formal series defining
P(\xi;u)
converges uniformly for
u\in\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(3r)
. First we note that for
u\in\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(3r)
we have
\mu(\xi;u)\leq 9r^{2}
and
y_{j}\leq 9r^{2}
for
j\in{\mathcal{S}}
. So by definition of the norm
\|P\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{cste}}}
it suffices to prove

\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathcal{S}}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathcal{S}}}\sum_{q\in\mathbb{N}}3^{2\|\boldsymbol{m}\|_{\ell^{1}}+2n}\ e^{-\eta(\|\boldsymbol{k}\|_{\ell^{1}}+2\|\boldsymbol{m}\|_{\ell^{1}}+q+2n)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{\ell}\in(\mathcal{S}^{c})^{q},\ \boldsymbol{\sigma}\in\{-1,1\}^{q}\\
\mathcal{M}(\boldsymbol{k},\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma})=0\end{subarray}}w_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}}\cdots w_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{q}}\,\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}1

where
w_{j}=|u_{j}|r^{-1}
for
j\in\mathcal{S}^{c}
and the momentum
\mathcal{M}(\boldsymbol{k},\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma})
is defined by

\mathcal{M}(\boldsymbol{k},\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}):=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{S}}j\boldsymbol{k}_{j}+\sum_{i=1}^{q}\sigma_{i}\ell_{i}.

Noticing that, since
s\geq 1
,
w\in\ell^{1}(\mathcal{S}^{c})
with
\|w\|_{\ell^{1}(\mathcal{S}^{c})}\leq 3r^{-1}\|u\|_{\ell_{\varpi}^{2}(\mathcal{S}^{c})}\leq 9
for
u\in\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(3r)
, we deduce

\sum_{\boldsymbol{\ell}\in(\mathcal{S}^{c})^{q},\ \boldsymbol{\sigma}\in\{-1,1\}^{q}}w_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}}\cdots w_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{q}}\,\leq(18)^{q}.

On the other hand the summability with respect to
\boldsymbol{k},q,n,\boldsymbol{m}
is ensured by the exponential
e^{-\eta(\|\boldsymbol{k}\|_{\ell^{1}}+2\|\boldsymbol{m}\|_{\ell^{1}}+q+2n)}
with
\eta\geq\eta_{0}
.
∎

Remark 3.5
.

The definition of the class
\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{cste}}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{S}}
has been chosen in such a way the function
u\mapsto P(\mu(\xi;u),y(\xi;u),z(u),u)
has good property. In particular,
in (
44
), the third term encoded the
\delta
-regularizing property inherit from Theorem
2.1
while the second term will be used to obtain a regularity property for the gradient: it maps
\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(3r)
into
\ell^{2}_{\varpi}
(see Lemma
3.8
).

Definition 3.6

(Hamiltonians with parameters
\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
)
.

Let
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{Z}
be a finite set,
r\in(0,\frac{1}{4})
,
\mathcal{O}\subset((4r)^{2};1)^{\mathcal{S}}
,
\eta\geq 0
. We denote by

\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}=:\mathrm{Lip}(\mathcal{O};\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{cste}})

the space of the Lipschitz functions from
\mathcal{O}
into the space of the formal series
\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{cste}}
. Naturally we equip
\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
with the norms

\|P\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}}=\sup_{\xi\in\mathcal{O}}\|P(\xi)\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{cste}}},\quad\|P\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{lip}}}=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi,\zeta\in\mathcal{O}\\
\xi\neq\zeta\end{subarray}}\frac{\|P(\xi)-P(\zeta)\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{cste}}}}{\|\xi-\zeta\|_{\ell^{1}}},

and

\|P\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}}:=\|P\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}}+r^{2}\|P\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{lip}}}.

Being given
\xi\in\mathcal{O}
, thanks to Lemma
3.4
, to shorten the notation, we set

P(\xi;u):=P(\xi)(\xi;u).

To avoid possible confusion, we denote by
P[\xi]:=P(\xi;\cdot)
the smooth function from
\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(3r)
into
\mathbb{R}
defined just above.

Finally, we define a useful projection to identify some terms which require more attention.

Definition 3.7

(Projection
\Pi_{n=0,\boldsymbol{m}=0}
)
.

Let
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{Z}
be a finite set,
r>0
and
\eta\geq 0
. We denote by
\Pi_{n=0,\boldsymbol{m}=0}
the projection defined (by restriction of the coefficients) for all
P\in\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{cste}}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{S}}

\Pi_{n=0,\boldsymbol{m}=0}P(\mu,y,z,v):=\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathcal{S}}}\sum_{q\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{\ell}\in(\mathcal{S}^{c})^{q}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}\in\{-1,1\}^{q}}P_{0,\boldsymbol{k},0}^{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\,v_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\,z^{\boldsymbol{k}}\,.

3.2.
Vector field estimates, Poisson brackets and flows

In this subsection, we prove all the basic estimates we will need later.

Lemma 3.8

(Vector field)
.

Let
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{Z}
be a finite set,
r\in(0,\frac{1}{4})
,
\mathcal{O}\subset((4r)^{2},1)^{\mathcal{S}}
,
\eta\geq\eta_{0}
,
\xi\in\mathcal{O}
and
P\in\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
. Then
\nabla P[\xi]
defines a smooth function from
\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(3r)
into
\ell^{2}_{\varpi}
and enjoys the estimate

\sup_{u\in\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(3r)}\|\nabla P(\xi;u)\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}r^{-2}\|P\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}},

\sup_{u\in\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(3r)}\|\mathrm{d}\nabla P(\xi;u)\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}\to\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}r^{-4}\|P\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}},

\sup_{u\in\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(3r)}\|\mathrm{d}^{2}\nabla P(\xi;u)\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}\times\ell^{2}_{\varpi}\to\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}r^{-6}\|P\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}}.

Moreover, if
\chi=\Pi_{n=0,\boldsymbol{m}=0}P
we have

\sup_{u\in\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(3r)}\|\nabla\chi(\xi;u)\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}r^{-1}\|P\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}},

\sup_{u\in\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(3r)}\|\mathrm{d}\nabla\chi(\xi;u)\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}\to\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}r^{-2}\|P\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}},

Proof.

We first consider
\nabla P
. The smoothness will be a consequence of the uniform convergence in
\ell^{2}_{\varpi}
of the formal series defining
\nabla P(\xi;u)
on
\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(3r)
, so we focus on the estimate of
\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\varpi_{j}^{2}|\partial_{\overline{u_{j}}}P(\xi;u)|^{2}
. We note that for
j,k\in\mathbb{Z}

\partial_{\overline{u_{j}}}\mu={u_{j}},\quad\partial_{\overline{u_{j}}}y_{k}=\delta_{j,k}{u_{j}},\quad\partial_{\overline{u_{j}}}z_{k}=-\delta_{j,k}\frac{z_{j}^{2}}{2|u_{j}|}

and thus for
j\in\mathcal{S}

\partial_{\overline{u_{j}}}P(\xi;u)={u_{j}}\partial_{\mu}P(\xi;u)+{u_{j}}\partial_{y_{j}}P(\xi;u)-\frac{z_{j}^{2}}{2|u_{j}|}\partial_{z_{j}}P(\xi;u)

and for
j\in\mathcal{S}^{c}

\partial_{\overline{u_{j}}}P(\xi;u)={u_{j}}\partial_{\mu}P(\xi;u)+\partial_{\overline{v_{j}}}P(\xi;u).

Since
\mathcal{S}
is finite, one easily obtains (note that
\partial_{\mu}P
and
\partial_{y_{j}}P
lose
r^{2}
and that
|u_{j}|\geq r
)

\sum_{j\in\mathcal{S}}\varpi_{j}^{2}|\partial_{\overline{u_{j}}}P(\xi;u)|^{2}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}r^{-2}\|P\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}}

by the same kind of estimates as in the proof of Lemma
3.4
. So we can focus on the sum over
\mathcal{S}^{c}
which is an infinite sum and for which we have to deal with the weight
\Theta_{\ell}
. Notice that using again Lemma
3.4
we have

\sum_{j\in\mathcal{S}^{c}}\varpi_{j}^{2}|{u_{j}}\partial_{\mu}P(\xi;u)|^{2}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}r^{-2}\|P\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}}.

Therefore it remains to estimate
\sum_{j\in\mathcal{S}^{c}}\varpi_{j}^{2}|\partial_{\overline{v_{j}}}P(\xi;u)|^{2}
.
To begin with, one has using the symmetry condition on the coefficients
P_{n,\boldsymbol{k},\boldsymbol{m}}^{\boldsymbol{\ell}\boldsymbol{\sigma}}
,

|\partial_{\overline{v_{j}}}P(\mu,y,z,v)|\leq\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathcal{S}}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathcal{S}}}\sum_{q\geq 1}q\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{\ell}\in(\mathcal{S}^{c})^{q},\ \boldsymbol{\sigma}\in\{-1,1\}^{q}\\
\mathcal{M}(\boldsymbol{k},(j,\boldsymbol{\ell}),(-1,\boldsymbol{\sigma}))=0\end{subarray}}\big|P_{n,\boldsymbol{k},\boldsymbol{m}}^{(j,\boldsymbol{\ell}),\boldsymbol{(1,\sigma})}\,v_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\,y^{\boldsymbol{m}}\,z^{\boldsymbol{k}}\,\mu^{n}\big|\,.

Notice that

\varpi_{j}\Theta_{(j,\boldsymbol{\ell})}\leq\varpi_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{*}_{1}}\varpi^{\flat}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{*}_{2}}\cdots\varpi^{\flat}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{*}_{q}}

where we recall that
\boldsymbol{\ell}^{*}_{1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{\ell}^{*}_{q}
is a rearrangement of
\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{\ell}_{q}
satisfying
|\boldsymbol{\ell}^{*}_{1}|\geq\cdots\geq|\boldsymbol{\ell}^{*}_{q}|
and
\varpi^{\flat}_{j}=\langle j\rangle^{-1}\varpi_{j}
.
Therefore, by definition of the norm
\|P\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}}
and of the domain
\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(3r)
, it suffices to prove

\sum_{j\in{\mathcal{S}}^{c}}\left|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathbb{N},\ \boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathcal{S}}\\
\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathcal{S}},\ q\geq 0\end{subarray}}q\,3^{2\|m\|_{\ell^{1}}+2n}\ e^{-\eta(\|\boldsymbol{k}\|_{\ell^{1}}+2\|\boldsymbol{m}\|_{\ell^{1}}+(q+1)+2n)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{\ell}\in(\mathcal{S}^{c})^{q},\ \boldsymbol{\sigma}\in\{-1,1\}^{q}\\
\mathcal{M}(\boldsymbol{k}(j,\boldsymbol{\ell}),(-1,\boldsymbol{\sigma}))=0\end{subarray}}t_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{*}_{1}}w_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{*}_{2}}\cdots w_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{*}_{q}}\,\right|^{2}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}1

where
w_{j}=\varpi^{\flat}_{j}|u_{j}|r^{-1}
and
t_{j}=\varpi_{j}|u_{j}|r^{-1}
for
j\in\mathcal{S}^{c}
.

First, since
\eta\geq\eta_{0}
, we can get rid of the sum with respect to
\boldsymbol{m}
and
n
using the exponential
e^{-\eta(2\|\boldsymbol{m}\|_{\ell^{1}}+2n)}
and then it remains to prove that

\sum_{j\in{\mathcal{S}}^{c}}\left|\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathcal{S}}}\sum_{q\geq 0}\ q\,e^{-\eta(\|\boldsymbol{k}\|_{\ell^{1}}+(q+1))}\ a^{(q,\boldsymbol{k})}_{j}\ \right|^{2}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}1

where

a^{(q,\boldsymbol{k})}_{j}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{\ell}\in(\mathcal{S}^{c})^{q},\ \boldsymbol{\sigma}\in\{-1,1\}^{q}\\
\mathcal{M}(\boldsymbol{k},(j,\boldsymbol{\ell}),(-1,\boldsymbol{\sigma}))=0\end{subarray}}t_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{*}_{1}}w_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{*}_{2}}\cdots w_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{*}_{q}}\leq q\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{\ell}\in(\mathcal{S}^{c})^{q},\ \boldsymbol{\sigma}\in\{-1,1\}^{q}\\
\mathcal{M}(\boldsymbol{k},(j,\boldsymbol{\ell}),(-1,\boldsymbol{\sigma}))=0\end{subarray}}t_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}}w_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2}}\cdots w_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{q}}.

We note that
t\in\ell^{2}(S^{c})
and
w\in\ell^{1}(S^{c})
with
\|w\|_{\ell^{1}},\ \|t\|_{\ell^{2}}\lesssim 1
for
u\in\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(3r)
. Therefore by Young convolutional inequality we get that

(a^{(q,\boldsymbol{k})}_{j})_{j\in{\mathcal{S}}^{c}}

belongs to
\ell^{2}
and we have uniformly in
q
and
\boldsymbol{k}

\|a^{(q,\boldsymbol{k})}\|_{\ell^{2}}\leq qC^{q}

for an universal constant
C
.
Thus, by triangular inequality, we have

\displaystyle\big(\sum_{j\in{\mathcal{S}}^{c}}

\displaystyle\big|\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathcal{S}}}\sum_{q\geq 0}q\,e^{-\eta(\|\boldsymbol{k}\|_{\ell^{1}}+q)}\ a^{(q,\boldsymbol{k})}_{j}\ \big|^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathcal{S}}}\sum_{q\geq 0}e^{-\eta(\|\boldsymbol{k}\|_{\ell^{1}}+q)}\big(\sum_{j\in{\mathcal{S}}^{c}}|a^{(q,\boldsymbol{k})}_{j}|^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}1\,.

The estimate of
\mathrm{d}\nabla P(\xi;u)
and
\mathrm{d}^{2}\nabla P(\xi;u)
are obtained in a similar way.
Moreover, the estimates concerning
\chi=\Pi_{n=0,\boldsymbol{m}=0}P
follow by noticing that only the derivatives with respect to
\mu
or to
y
lose
r^{2}
, the derivatives with respect to
u
or to
z
lose only
r
.
∎

We also have to control flows of Hamiltonians in
\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
.

Lemma 3.9

(Flows)
.

Let
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{Z}
be a finite set,
r\in(0,\frac{1}{4})
,
\mathcal{O}\subset((4r)^{2},1)^{\mathcal{S}}
,
\eta\geq\eta_{0}
and
\chi\in\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
. Assume that

(45)

\|\chi\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}r^{4},

or, in the case
\chi=\Pi_{n=0,\boldsymbol{m}=0}\chi
,

(46)

\|\chi\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}r^{2}.

Assume also that

(47)

r\lesssim_{{\mathcal{S}}}1.

Then, for all
\xi\in\mathcal{O}
, the flow of the equation
\mathrm{i}\partial_{t}v=\nabla\chi(\xi;v)
defines a smooth map
\Phi^{t}_{\chi}(\xi;\cdot)\equiv\Phi_{\chi}^{t}(\cdot):(t,u)\in[-1,1]\times\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(2r)\to\ell^{2}_{\varpi}
enjoying the following properties:

(i)

for each
t\in[-1,1]
,
\Phi_{\chi}^{t}
defines a symplectic change of variable from
\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(2r)
onto an open set of
\ell^{2}_{\varpi}
included in
\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(3r)
,

(ii)

for all
r\leq\rho\leq 2r
,
\Phi_{\chi}^{1}
maps
\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(\rho)
into
\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(\rho^{\prime})
with
0<\rho^{\prime}-\rho\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}r^{-2}\|\chi\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}}
.

(iii)

\Phi_{\chi}^{t}
and
\mathrm{d}\Phi_{\chi}^{t}
are close to the identity: for all
u\in\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(2r)
, all
t\in[0,1]

\|\Phi_{\chi}^{t}(u)-u\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}+r^{2}\|\mathrm{d}\Phi_{\chi}^{t}(u)-{\rm Id}\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}\to\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}r^{-2}\|\chi\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}}

and, in the case
\chi=\Pi_{n=0,\boldsymbol{m}=0}\chi
,

\|\Phi_{\chi}^{t}(u)-u\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}+r\|\mathrm{d}\Phi_{\chi}^{t}(u)-{\rm Id}\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}\to\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}r^{-1}\|\chi\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}},

(iv)

\mathrm{d}^{2}\Phi_{\chi}^{t}
is bounded: for all
u\in\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(2r)
, all
t\in[0,1]

\|\mathrm{d}^{2}\Phi_{\chi}^{t}(u)\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}\times\ell^{2}_{\varpi}\to\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}r^{-6}\|\chi\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}}.

Proof.

Since, by Lemma
3.8
, the vector field
\nabla\chi
is locally-Lipschitz, the local existence and the smoothness of the flow
\Phi_{\chi}^{t}
is ensured by the Cauchy-Lipchitz Theorem. So, to prove assertion (i), we now have to check that the flow exists for
|t|\leq 1
. Without loss of generality we only consider positive times. More precisely, let
T>0
and
v\in C^{1}([0,T);\ell^{2}_{\varpi})
be a solution of the Cauchy problem

-\mathrm{i}\partial_{t}v(t)=\nabla\chi(\xi;v(t))\quad\mathrm{and}\quad v(0)=u\in\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(2r).

It suffices to prove that if
0\leq t<T
and
t\leq 1
then
v(t)\in\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(\frac{5}{2}r)
. We set
I=[0,T)\cap[0,1]
and we aim at proving that
J=I
where

J=\big\{t\in I\ |\ \forall\tau\in[0,t],\ v(\tau)\in\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(\frac{5}{2}r)\big\}.

Since
v
is continuous,
J
is clearly non-empty and closed in
I
. Moreover, if
t\in J
then, using Lemma
3.8
,

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\big||v_{k}(t)|^{2}-\xi_{k}\big|\varpi_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\big||v_{k}(0)|^{2}-\xi_{k}\big|\varpi_{k}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2|\partial_{t}v_{k}(\tau)||v_{k}(\tau)|\varpi_{k}^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq 4r^{2}+2\sup_{w\in\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(3r)}\big(\|\nabla\chi(\xi;w)\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\|w\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\big)\,.

Thus, using that
w\in\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(3r)
implies
\|w\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}1
, we get

\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\big||v_{k}(t)|^{2}-\xi_{k}\big|\varpi_{k}^{2}-4r^{2}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}r^{-2}\|\chi\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}}.

Then, using (
45
) and (
47
), we get
v(t)\in\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(\frac{5}{2}r)
for all
t\in J
. Therefore, since
v
is continuous,
J
is open and so, since
I
is connected, we have
J=I
. Moreover, we notice that, when
\chi=\Pi_{n=0,\boldsymbol{m}=0}\chi
, Lemma
3.8
gives us a better estimate and the same conclusion remains true under the hypothesis (
46
).
Since the symplecticity is ensured by the fact that
\Phi_{\chi}
is a Hamiltonian flow, we have proved assertion (i).

Similarly, if initially
v(0)=u\in\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(\rho)
then we get

\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\big||v_{k}(t)|^{2}-\xi_{k}\big|\varpi_{k}^{2}-\rho^{2}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}r^{-2}\|\chi\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}}

and therefore
v(1)\in\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(\rho^{\prime})
with
|(\rho^{\prime})^{2}-\rho^{2}|\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}r^{-2}\|\chi\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}}.

To prove assertion (iii) we note that, by Lemma
3.8
,

\displaystyle\|v(t)-u\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\leq\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{w\in\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(3r)}\|\nabla\chi(\xi;w)\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\mathrm{d}\tau\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}r^{-2}\|\chi\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}}.

Now we focus on
\mathrm{d}\Phi_{\chi}^{t}(u)
. If
w\in\ell^{2}_{\varpi}
, we have

(48)

-\mathrm{i}\partial_{t}\mathrm{d}\Phi_{\chi}^{t}(u)(w)=\mathrm{d}\nabla\chi(v(t))(\mathrm{d}\Phi_{\chi}^{t}(u)(w))

and thus by Lemma
3.8

\|\mathrm{d}\Phi_{\chi}^{t}(u)(w)-w\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\leq\!\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathrm{d}\nabla\chi(v(\tau))(\mathrm{d}\Phi_{\chi}^{\tau}(u)(w))\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\mathrm{d}\tau\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}r^{-4}\|\chi\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}}\!\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathrm{d}\Phi_{\chi}^{\tau}(u)(w)\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\mathrm{d}\tau.

Therefore, we get by Grönwall’s inequality:

\|\mathrm{d}\Phi_{\chi}^{t}(u)(w)\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\leq\|w\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\mathrm{exp}(C_{S}r^{-4}\|\chi\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}}t).

for all
t\in[0,1]
where
C_{\mathcal{S}}>0
is a constant depending on
{\mathcal{S}}
. Using hypothesis (
45
), we get that for all
t\in[0,1]
,
\|\mathrm{d}\Phi_{\chi}^{t}(u)(w)\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\leq 2\|w\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}
.
Then we deduce

\|\mathrm{d}\Phi_{\chi}^{t}(u)(w)-w\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}r^{-4}\|\chi\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}}\|w\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}.

Again we notice that, when
\chi=\Pi_{n=0,\boldsymbol{m}=0}\chi
, Lemma
3.8
gives us a better estimate which leads to better estimates of
\|\Phi_{\chi}^{t}(u)-u\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}+r\|\mathrm{d}\Phi_{\chi}^{t}(u)-\rm{Id}\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}\to\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}
as stated in (iii).

To prove (iv) we derive once again (
48
) to get for
w,w^{\prime}\in\ell^{2}_{\varpi}

-i\partial_{t}\mathrm{d}^{2}\Phi_{\chi}^{t}(u)(w,w^{\prime})=\mathrm{d}^{2}\nabla\chi(v(t))(\mathrm{d}\Phi_{\chi}^{t}(u)(w),\mathrm{d}\Phi_{\chi}^{t}(u)(w^{\prime}))+\mathrm{d}\nabla\chi(v(t))(\mathrm{d}^{2}\Phi_{\chi}^{t}(u)(w,w^{\prime}))

and thus by Lemma
3.8

\|\mathrm{d}^{2}\Phi_{\chi}^{t}(u)(w,w^{\prime})\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}r^{-6}\|\chi\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}}+r^{-4}\|\chi\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}}\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathrm{d}^{2}\Phi_{\chi}^{\tau}(u)(w,w^{\prime})\|_{\ell^{2}_{\varpi}}\mathrm{d}\tau\,.

Then we conclude by Grönwall’s inequality.
∎

Now we want to estimate the Poisson bracket between to elements of
\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
but first we prove a technical lemma that we will need.

Lemma 3.10
.

Let
{\mathcal{S}}\subset\mathbb{Z}
,
q,q^{\prime}\geq 0
and
q^{\prime\prime}=q+q^{\prime}
. Let
({\mathcal{S}}^{c})^{q^{\prime\prime}}\ni\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}=(\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime})\in({\mathcal{S}}^{c})^{q}\times({\mathcal{S}}^{c})^{q^{\prime}}
and
j\in{\mathcal{S}}^{c}
then

(49)

\Theta_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}}^{-1}\leq\Theta_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{-1}\Theta_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime}}^{-1}

and

(50)

\Theta_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}}^{-1}\leq\Theta_{(\boldsymbol{\ell},j)}^{-1}\Theta_{(\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime},j)}^{-1}\ .

Proof.

Without loss of generality we can assume that
\boldsymbol{\ell}
,
\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime}
and
\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}
are ordered and non negative (i.e.
\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}\geq\cdots\boldsymbol{\ell}_{q}\geq 0
and the same for
\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime}
and
\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}
) and that
j
is non negative. The definition of
\Theta_{\ell}
(see (
44
)) contains two special cases
q=0,1
. In this proof we will consider only the generic case, i.e.
q,q^{\prime}\geq 2
, all the remaining special cases are treated similarly (i.e. without extra trick).
First we focus on (
49
), we recall that by definition (since we assume
q\geq 2
)

\Theta_{\ell}=1\ \wedge\ \varpi^{\flat}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{3}}\cdots\varpi^{\flat}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{q}}\frac{\varpi_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2}}}{\varpi_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}}}\ \wedge\ \frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}\rangle^{2\delta}\cdots\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}_{q}\rangle^{2\delta}}{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}\rangle^{5\delta}}\ ,

where
\varpi^{\flat}_{k}=\langle k\rangle^{-1}\varpi_{k}
.
To begin with, let us denote

c_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}=\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}\rangle^{2}\cdots\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}_{q}\rangle^{2}}{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}\rangle^{5}}.

Assuming for instance
{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}^{\prime\prime}}={\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{1}
(the case
{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}^{\prime\prime}}=\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}^{\prime}
is treated in the same way), we have

\displaystyle c_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}\rangle^{2}\cdots\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}_{q}\rangle^{2}}{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}\rangle^{5}}\ {\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime}_{1}\rangle^{2}\cdots\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime}_{q^{\prime}}\rangle^{2}}\geq\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}\rangle^{2}\cdots\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}_{q}\rangle^{2}}{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}\rangle^{5}}\ \frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime}_{1}\rangle^{2}\cdots\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime}_{q^{\prime}}\rangle^{2}}{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime}_{1}\rangle^{5}}=c_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}c_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime}}\,.

So it remains to prove that

\varpi^{\flat}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{3}}\cdots\varpi^{\flat}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{q^{\prime\prime}}}\frac{\varpi_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{2}}}{\varpi_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}}\geq\varpi^{\flat}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{3}}\cdots\varpi^{\flat}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{q}}\frac{\varpi_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2}}}{\varpi_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}}}\ \varpi^{\flat}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime}_{3}}\cdots\varpi^{\flat}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime}_{q^{\prime}}}\frac{\varpi_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime}_{2}}}{\varpi_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime}_{1}}}\,.

We first notice that

\varpi^{\flat}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{3}}\cdots\varpi^{\flat}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{q}}\frac{\varpi_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2}}}{\varpi_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}}}=a_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\ b_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}

where

a_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}=\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{q}\varpi^{\flat}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{i}}}{(\varpi^{\flat}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}})^{2}}\quad\text{and}\quad b_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}=\frac{\langle{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{2}\rangle}{\langle{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{1}\rangle}.

We note for further use that
b_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}\leq 1
.
In the same way than we estimate
c_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}}
we easily get

a_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}}\geq a_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}a_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime}}.

On the other hand, when
{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}^{\prime\prime}}={\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{1}
then
b_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}}\geq b_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}
and thus using
b_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime}}\leq 1
we obtain

b_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}}\geq b_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}b_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime}}.

The case
{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{1}}={\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\prime}_{1}
is handled in the same way. This conclude the proof of (
49
).

Now we focus on (
50
). We verify that

c_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}}=c_{(\boldsymbol{\ell},j)}c_{(\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime},j)}\frac{\max(\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}\rangle,\langle j\rangle)^{5}\ \max(\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime}_{1}\rangle,\langle j\rangle)^{5}}{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\rangle^{5}\langle j\rangle^{4}}

which leads to
c_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}}\geq c_{(\boldsymbol{\ell},j)}c_{(\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime},j)}
.
Now it suffices to prove that

a_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}}\geq a_{(\boldsymbol{\ell},j)}a_{(\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime},j)}\text{ and }\quad b_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}}\geq b_{(\boldsymbol{\ell},j)}b_{(\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime},j)}.

We begin with we prove the second estimate:

—

If
\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\leq j
then

b_{(\boldsymbol{\ell},j)}b_{(\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime},j)}=\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2}\rangle}{\langle j\rangle}\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime}_{2}\rangle}{\langle j\rangle}\leq\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2}\rangle}{\langle j\rangle}\leq\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\rangle}{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\rangle}=b_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}}.

—

If
\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\leq j\leq\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
then either
\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{1}=\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}
and thus
b_{(\boldsymbol{\ell},j)}=\frac{\langle j\rangle}{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\rangle}
,
b_{(\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime},j)}=\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime}_{1}\rangle}{\langle j\rangle}\leq\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\rangle}{\langle j\rangle}
, or
\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{1}=\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime}_{1}
and thus
b_{(\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime},j)}=\frac{\langle j\rangle}{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\rangle}
,
b_{(\boldsymbol{\ell},j)}=\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}\rangle}{\langle j\rangle}\leq\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\rangle}{\langle j\rangle}
, which in both cases leads to

b_{(\boldsymbol{\ell},j)}b_{(\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime},j)}\leq\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\rangle}{\langle j\rangle}\frac{\langle j\rangle}{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\rangle}=b_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}}.

—

If
j\leq\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
then either
\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}=\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
and then

b_{(\boldsymbol{\ell},j)}=\frac{\max(\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2}\rangle,\langle j\rangle)}{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\rangle}\leq\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\rangle}{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\rangle}=b_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}}
or
\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime}_{1}=\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
and then

b_{(\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime},j)}=\frac{\max(\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime}_{2}\rangle,\langle j\rangle)}{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\rangle}\leq\frac{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\rangle}{\langle\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{1}\rangle}=b_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}}
. In both cases we get

b_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}}\geq b_{(\boldsymbol{\ell},j)}b_{(\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime},j)}.

Finally it remains to prove
a_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}}\geq a_{(\boldsymbol{\ell},j)}a_{(\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime},j)}
:

a_{(\boldsymbol{\ell},j)}a_{(\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime},j)}=\frac{\big(\prod_{i=1}^{q^{\prime\prime}}\varpi^{\flat}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{i}}\big)(\varpi^{\flat}_{j})^{2}}{\max(\varpi^{\flat}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}_{1}},\varpi^{\flat}_{j})^{2}\max(\varpi^{\flat}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime}_{1}},\varpi^{\flat}_{j})^{2}}\leq\frac{\big(\prod_{i=1}^{q^{\prime\prime}}\varpi^{\flat}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{i}}\big)(\varpi^{\flat}_{j})^{2}}{(\varpi^{\flat}_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}_{1}})^{2}(\varpi^{\flat}_{j})^{2}}=a_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}}.

∎

We define in
\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{cste}}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{S}}
the projection on the total degree by

\Pi_{p}P:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathbb{N},\ \boldsymbol{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\mathcal{S}},\ \boldsymbol{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\mathcal{S}},\ q\in\mathbb{N}\\
\|\boldsymbol{k}\|_{\ell^{1}}+2\|\boldsymbol{m}\|_{\ell^{1}}+q+2n=p\end{subarray}}\ \ \sum_{\boldsymbol{\ell}\in(\mathcal{S}^{c})^{q}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}\in\{-1,1\}^{q}}P_{n,\boldsymbol{k},\boldsymbol{m}}^{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\,v_{\boldsymbol{\ell}}^{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\,y^{\boldsymbol{m}}\,z^{\boldsymbol{k}}\,\mu^{n}.

About Poisson brackets we have

Lemma 3.11

(Poisson brackets)
.

Let
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{Z}
be a finite set,
r\in(0,\frac{1}{4})
,
\mathcal{O}\subset((4r)^{2},1)^{\mathcal{S}}
,
\eta>\eta_{0}
,
\beta>0

\xi\in\mathcal{O}
,
P,Q\in\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
. There exists a unique
R\in\mathscr{H}_{\eta-{\alpha},r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
for any
0<\alpha<\eta-\eta_{0}
such that

\{P(\xi;\cdot),Q(\xi;\cdot)\}=R(\xi;\cdot)\quad\text{on }\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(3r)

and

(51)

\|R\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta-\alpha,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}\ \ \frac{1}{r^{2}{\alpha}^{4}}\|P\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}}\|Q\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}}.

If furthermore
Q\in\mathscr{H}_{\eta+\beta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
for some
\beta>0
then
for any
p\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

(52)

\|\Pi_{p}R\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta-{\alpha},r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}\ \ \frac{(p+1)e^{-{\alpha}p}}{r^{2}\beta^{4}}\|P\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}}\|Q\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta+\beta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}}.

Since
R
is unique from now on we set
\{P,Q\}:=R
.

Proof.

To keep the notations simple, we identify, for fixed
\xi
, the formal series
P
and the function
P(\xi;u)
.
By definition, using that
P
and
Q
commute with
\mu
(conservation of the mass), we have

\{P,Q\}=\underbrace{\sum_{j\in{\mathcal{S}}}\partial_{\theta_{j}}P\partial_{y_{j}}Q-\partial_{y_{j}}Q\partial_{\theta_{j}}P}_{K}\ +\ 2\mathrm{i}\underbrace{\sum_{j\in{\mathcal{S}}^{c}}\partial_{\overline{v_{j}}}P\partial_{v_{j}}Q-\partial_{\overline{v_{j}}}Q\partial_{v_{j}}P}_{L}.

First we consider the finite sum. We have, taking into account the symmetry condition (here
q^{\prime\prime}
denotes the cardinality of
\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}
)

(53)

\sharp(\mathfrak{S}_{q^{\prime\prime}})K_{n^{\prime\prime},\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime\prime},\boldsymbol{m}^{\prime\prime}}^{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime},\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}}=\sum_{\varphi\in\mathfrak{S}_{q^{\prime\prime}}}\tilde{K}_{n^{\prime\prime},\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime\prime},\boldsymbol{m}^{\prime\prime}}^{\varphi\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime},\varphi\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}}

where

\displaystyle\tilde{K}_{n^{\prime\prime},\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime\prime},\boldsymbol{m}^{\prime\prime}}^{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime},\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}}=\mathrm{i}\sum_{j\in{\mathcal{S}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}=(\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime}),\ \boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}=(\boldsymbol{\sigma},\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime}),\\
\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime\prime}=\boldsymbol{k}+\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime},\ \boldsymbol{m}^{\prime\prime}=\boldsymbol{m}+\boldsymbol{m}^{\prime},\ n^{\prime\prime}=n+n^{\prime}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{k}_{j}(\boldsymbol{m}^{\prime}_{j}+1)P_{n,\boldsymbol{k},\boldsymbol{m}}^{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}Q_{n^{\prime},\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime},(\boldsymbol{m}^{\prime}+e_{j})}^{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle-\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}_{j}(1+\boldsymbol{m}_{j})P_{n,\boldsymbol{k},(\boldsymbol{m}+e_{j})}^{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}Q_{n^{\prime},\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{m}^{\prime}}^{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime}}

where
(e_{j})_{j\in S}
denotes the canonical basis of
\mathbb{R}^{S}
. The two terms of the right hand side are essentially the same, it suffices to switch
P
and
Q
. So we focus on the first term and we note that the sum with respect to
j
is finite and thus not important. Further, remembering that we want to estimate
\Pi_{p^{\prime\prime}}K
for
p^{\prime\prime}\geq 0
, we finally have to estimate

I_{j}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}=(\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime}),\ \boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}=(\boldsymbol{\sigma},\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime}),\\
\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime\prime}=\boldsymbol{k}+\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime},\ \boldsymbol{m}^{\prime\prime}=\boldsymbol{m}+\boldsymbol{m}^{\prime},\ n^{\prime\prime}=n+n^{\prime}\\
\|\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime\prime}\|_{\ell^{1}}+2\|\boldsymbol{m}^{\prime\prime}\|_{\ell^{1}}+q^{\prime\prime}+n^{\prime\prime}=p^{\prime\prime}\end{subarray}}|\boldsymbol{k}_{j}|(\boldsymbol{m}^{\prime}_{j}+1)\big|P_{n,\boldsymbol{k},\boldsymbol{m}}^{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\big|\ \big|Q_{n^{\prime},\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime},(\boldsymbol{m}^{\prime}+e_{j})}^{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime}}\big|.

Let
p=\|\boldsymbol{k}\|_{\ell^{1}}+2\|\boldsymbol{m}\|_{\ell^{1}}+q+n
,
p^{\prime}=\|\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}\|_{\ell^{1}}+2\|\boldsymbol{m}^{\prime}\|_{\ell^{1}}+q^{\prime}+n^{\prime}+2
(here
q
,
q^{\prime}
denote as usual the cardinality of
\boldsymbol{\ell}
,
\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime}
respectively). Since
n^{\prime\prime}=n+n^{\prime}
,
\|\boldsymbol{m}^{\prime\prime}\|_{\ell^{1}}=\|\boldsymbol{m}^{\prime}\|_{\ell^{1}}+\|\boldsymbol{m}\|_{\ell^{1}}
,
q^{\prime\prime}=q+q^{\prime}
and
\|\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime\prime}\|_{\ell^{1}}-\|\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}\|_{\ell^{1}}\leq\|\boldsymbol{k}\|_{\ell^{1}}\leq\|\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime\prime}\|_{\ell^{1}}+\|\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}\|_{\ell^{1}}
,
we get
p^{\prime\prime}\leq p+p^{\prime}-2
and also
p\leq p^{\prime}+p^{\prime\prime}
. Thus, we have

I_{j}\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\|\boldsymbol{k}\|_{\ell^{1}}+2\|\boldsymbol{m}\|_{\ell^{1}}+q+n=p\\
\|\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}\|_{\ell^{1}}+2\|\boldsymbol{m}^{\prime}\|_{\ell^{1}}+q^{\prime}+n^{\prime}+2=p^{\prime}\\
p^{\prime\prime}-p^{\prime}+2\leq p\leq p^{\prime\prime}+p^{\prime}\end{subarray}}pp^{\prime}\big|P_{n,\boldsymbol{k},\boldsymbol{m}}^{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\big|\ \big|Q_{n^{\prime},\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{m}^{\prime}}^{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime}}\big|.

Therefore, using Lemma
3.10
, we deduce

\|\Pi_{p^{\prime\prime}}\tilde{K}\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\eta-{\alpha},r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}r^{-2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p,p^{\prime}\geq 1\\
p^{\prime\prime}-p^{\prime}+2\leq p\leq p^{\prime\prime}+p^{\prime}\end{subarray}}pp^{\prime}\|\Pi_{p}P\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\eta-{\alpha},r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}\|\Pi_{p^{\prime}}Q\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\eta-{\alpha},r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}

and since our previous estimates are uniform with respect to permutation on
\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}
and
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}
we conclude

(54)

\|\Pi_{p^{\prime\prime}}K\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\eta-{\alpha},r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}r^{-2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p,p^{\prime}\geq 1\\
p^{\prime\prime}-p^{\prime}+2\leq p\leq p^{\prime\prime}+p^{\prime}\end{subarray}}pp^{\prime}\|\Pi_{p}P\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\eta-{\alpha},r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}\|\Pi_{p^{\prime}}Q\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\eta-{\alpha},r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}.

Now we focus on the infinite sum
L
. Following the same strategy as above (i.e. a decomposition of
L
as in (
53
)), we have to estimate

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime\prime}=(\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime}),\ \boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}=(\boldsymbol{\sigma},\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime}),\\
\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime\prime}=\boldsymbol{k}+\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime},\,\boldsymbol{m}^{\prime\prime}=\boldsymbol{m}+\boldsymbol{m}^{\prime},\\
n^{\prime\prime}=n+n^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\hskip-28.45274pt(q+1)(q^{\prime}+1)\mathbbm{1}_{j^{*}\in\mathcal{S}^{c}}\left(\big|P_{n,\boldsymbol{k},\boldsymbol{m}}^{(\boldsymbol{\ell},j^{*}),(\boldsymbol{\sigma},+1)}\,Q_{n^{\prime},\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{m}^{\prime}}^{(\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime},j^{*}),(\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime},-1)}\big|+\big|P_{n,\boldsymbol{k},\boldsymbol{m}}^{(\boldsymbol{\ell},j^{*}),(\boldsymbol{\sigma},-1)}\,Q_{n^{\prime},\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{m}^{\prime}}^{(\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime},j^{*}),(\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime},+1)}\big|\right)

where
q
and
q^{\prime}
denote the cardinality of
\boldsymbol{\ell}
and
\boldsymbol{\ell}^{\prime}
respectively and, as aconsequence of the zero momentum condition,

-j^{*}=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{S}}j\boldsymbol{k}_{j}+\sum_{i=1}^{q}\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{i}\boldsymbol{\ell}_{i}.

So we obtain, using again Lemma
3.10
, the same estimate for
L
than estimate (
54
) for
K
and all together we get

(55)

\|\Pi_{p^{\prime\prime}}R\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\eta-{\alpha},r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}r^{-2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p,p^{\prime}\geq 1\\
p^{\prime\prime}-p^{\prime}+2\leq p\leq p^{\prime\prime}+p^{\prime}\end{subarray}}pp^{\prime}\|\Pi_{p}P\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\eta-{\alpha},r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}\|\Pi_{p^{\prime}}Q\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\eta-{\alpha},r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}.

Now we prove (
51
). We use
\|\Pi_{p}\cdot\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\eta-{\alpha},r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}\leq e^{-\alpha p}\|\cdot\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}
to get from (
55
)

\|\Pi_{p^{\prime\prime}}R\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\eta-{\alpha},r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}r^{-2}\|P\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}\|Q\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}\sum_{p,p^{\prime}\geq 1}pp^{\prime}e^{-\alpha p}e^{-{\alpha}p^{\prime}}.

Then we use that
\sum_{p\geq 1}pe^{-{\alpha}p}\leq 2\int_{0}^{+\infty}te^{-{\alpha}t}\mathrm{d}t=\frac{2}{{\alpha}^{2}}
, to conclude that, uniformly in
p^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
,

\|\Pi_{p^{\prime\prime}}R\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\eta-{\alpha},r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}\frac{1}{r^{2}{\alpha}^{4}}\|P\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}\|Q\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}.

We also get for all
p\geq 0

\|\Pi_{p}R\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{lip}}_{\eta-{\alpha},r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}\ \frac{1}{r^{2}{\alpha}^{4}}\left(\|P\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{lip}}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}\|Q\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}+\|P\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}\|Q\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{lip}}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}\right)

and thus (
51
) follows.

Now we prove (
52
).
We use
\|\Pi_{p^{\prime}}\cdot\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\eta-{\alpha},r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}\leq e^{-({\alpha}+\beta)p^{\prime}}\|\cdot\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\eta+\beta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}
to get from (
55
)

\|\Pi_{p^{\prime\prime}}R\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\eta-{\alpha},r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}r^{-2}\|P\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}\|Q\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\eta+\beta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p,p^{\prime}\geq 1\\
p^{\prime\prime}-p^{\prime}+2\leq p\leq p^{\prime\prime}+p^{\prime}\end{subarray}}pp^{\prime}e^{-\alpha p}e^{-(\beta+{\alpha})p^{\prime}}.

We have
e^{-\alpha p}\leq e^{-\alpha p^{\prime\prime}}e^{\alpha p^{\prime}}
and thus

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p,p^{\prime}\geq 1\\
p^{\prime\prime}-p^{\prime}+2\leq p\leq p^{\prime\prime}+p^{\prime}\end{subarray}}pp^{\prime}e^{-\alpha p}e^{-(\beta+{\alpha})p^{\prime}}\leq\sum_{p^{\prime}\geq 1}2p^{\prime}(p^{\prime\prime}+p^{\prime})p^{\prime}e^{-\alpha p^{\prime\prime}}e^{-\beta p^{\prime}}\leq 4(p^{\prime\prime}+1)e^{-\alpha p^{\prime\prime}}\sum_{p^{\prime}\geq 1}p^{\prime 3}e^{-\beta p^{\prime}}.

Finally we use
\sum_{p^{\prime}\geq 1}p^{\prime 3}e^{-\beta p^{\prime}}\leq\frac{12}{\beta^{4}}
to conclude

\|\Pi_{p}R\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\eta-{\alpha},r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}\frac{(p+1)e^{-\alpha p}}{r^{2}\beta^{4}}\|P\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}\|Q\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\eta+\beta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}.

This prove (
52
) for the
sup
norm. We also get

\|\Pi_{p}R\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{lip}}_{\eta-{\alpha},r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}\ \frac{(p+1)e^{-\alpha p}}{r^{2}\beta^{4}}\left(\|P\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{lip}}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}\|Q\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\eta+\beta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}+\|P\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{sup}}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}\|Q\|_{\mathscr{H}^{\mathrm{lip}}_{\eta+\beta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}}\right)

from which (
52
) follows.

∎

We end this section with a result on the action of the Hamiltonian flow
\Phi_{\chi}^{t}
by composition.

Lemma 3.12
.

Let
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{Z}
be a finite set,
r\in(0,\frac{1}{4})
,
\mathcal{O}\subset((4r)^{2},1)^{\mathcal{S}}
,
\eta_{\mathrm{\max}}>\eta>\eta_{0}
,
\alpha\in(0,\eta-\eta_{0})
and
\chi\in\mathscr{H}_{\eta+{\alpha},r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
. Assume that
\chi
satisfies (
45
) (or (
46
) if
\chi=\Pi_{n=0,\boldsymbol{m}=0}\chi
) and

(56)

\|\chi\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta+\alpha,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}\alpha^{5}r^{2}.

Assume also that
r
satisfies (
47
).
Then for any
H\in\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
and any
t\in[0,1]
, there exits
H_{t}\in\mathscr{H}_{\eta-\alpha,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
such that, for all
\xi\in\mathcal{O}
,
H(\xi;\cdot)\circ\Phi^{t}_{\chi}(\xi;\cdot)=H_{t}(\xi;\cdot)
on
\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(2r)

and, identifying the formal series and the functions
16
16

16

i.e. identifying
H\circ\Phi^{t}_{\chi}
and
H_{t}
, we have

(57)

\|H\circ\Phi^{t}_{\chi}\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta-\alpha,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}}\leq 2\ \|H\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}},

(58)

\|H\circ\Phi^{t}_{\chi}-H\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta-\alpha,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}\ \frac{1}{r^{2}{\alpha}^{5}}\|\chi\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta+{\alpha},r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}}\|H\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}}

and

(59)

\|H\circ\Phi^{1}_{\chi}-H-\{H,\chi\}\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta-\alpha,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}\ \frac{1}{r^{4}{\alpha}^{10}}\|\chi\|^{2}_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta+{\alpha},r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}}\|H\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}}

where, with a slight abuse of notation, we have written
H\circ\Phi^{t}_{\chi}
instead of
H(\xi;\cdot)\circ\Phi^{t}_{\chi}(\xi;\cdot)
.

Proof.

We want to apply the Cauchy–Kowaleskaya lemma proven in the appendix (see Lemma
10.1
). So we set
E^{(p+1)}=\Pi_{p}\mathscr{H}_{\eta_{0},r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
and
\|\cdot\|_{E^{(p+1)}}=\|\cdot\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta_{0},r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}}
.
We note that, with the notation of Lemma
10.1
, we have for any
\beta\geq 0

E_{\beta}=\mathscr{H}_{\eta_{0}+\beta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\|\cdot\|_{\beta}=\|\cdot\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta_{0}+\beta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}}.

By Lemma
3.11
we have for all
0<\beta\leq\eta-\eta_{0}

\|\Pi_{p}\{H,\chi\}\|_{E^{(p+1)}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}\ \frac{(p+1)e^{-\beta(p+1)}}{r^{2}{\alpha}^{4}}\|H\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\beta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}}\|\chi\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta+{\alpha},r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}}

and thus the linear map
LH:=\{H,\chi\}
satisfies (
173
) with
C_{L}:=c(S)\|\chi\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta+\alpha,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}}(r^{2}{\alpha}^{4})^{-1}
. Note that, as a consequence of (
56
), we have
2C_{L}\leq{\alpha}
.

Now let
H\in\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
. Applying Lemma
10.1
, there exists
t\mapsto H_{t}\in C^{1}([0,1];\mathscr{H}_{\eta-{\alpha},r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}})
solution of the transport equation

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\partial_{t}H_{t}=\{H_{t},\chi\},\quad t\in[0,1]\\
H(0)=H.\end{array}\right..

Consider
g(t)=H_{t}\circ\Phi_{\chi}^{-t}(u)
for some
u\in\mathcal{A}_{\xi}(2r)
. We have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}g(t)

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}H_{t}\circ\Phi_{\chi}^{-t}(u)+\mathrm{d}H_{t}(\Phi_{\chi}^{-t}(u))(\partial_{t}\Phi_{\chi}^{-t}(u))

\displaystyle=\{H_{t},\chi\}\circ\Phi_{\chi}^{-t}(u)-(\mathrm{i}\nabla H_{t}(\Phi_{\chi}^{-t}(u)),\nabla\chi(\Phi_{\chi}^{-t}(u)))_{L^{2}}=0

and thus
H_{t}\circ\Phi_{\chi}^{-t}(u)=g(t)=g(0)=H(u)
. Therefore
H_{t}=H\circ\Phi_{\chi}^{t}
and we deduce that
H\circ\Phi_{\chi}^{t}
belongs to
\mathscr{H}_{\eta-\alpha,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}
for all
t\in[0,1]
and satisfies (
57
).
To prove (
58
) we write

H\circ\Phi^{t}_{\chi}-H=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}(H\circ\Phi_{\chi}^{\tau})\mathrm{d}\tau

and we use
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}(H\circ\Phi_{\chi}^{\tau})=\{H,\chi\}\circ\Phi_{\chi}^{\tau}
to conclude using an adapted version of (
57
) with
\eta
replaced by
\eta-\frac{{\alpha}}{2}
and
{\alpha}
replaced by
\frac{{\alpha}}{2}
(and thus adapting (
47
))

\|H\circ\Phi^{t}_{\chi}-H\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta-{\alpha},r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}}\leq 2\ \|\{H,\chi\}\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta-\frac{{\alpha}}{2},r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}}.

Then we use (
52
) and the uniform estimate
(p+1)e^{-\frac{{\alpha}}{2}p}\leq\frac{8}{{\alpha}}
to get

\|\{H,\chi\}\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta-\frac{{\alpha}}{2},r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{S}}\frac{1}{r^{2}{\alpha}^{5}}\|\chi\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta+{\alpha},r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}}\|H\|_{\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}}

which completes the proof of (
58
). The proof of (
59
) is obtained similarly, writing

H\circ\Phi^{1}_{\chi}-H-\{H,\chi\}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}t^{2}}(H\circ\Phi_{\chi}^{t})\mathrm{d}t

and using
\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}t^{2}}(H\circ\Phi_{\chi}^{t})=\{\{H,\chi\},\chi\}\circ\Phi_{\chi}^{t}
.

∎

3.3.
Spaces of integrable Hamiltonians

The integrable part of Hamiltonians plays an important role in our context, we cannot eliminate them by normal forms and they nature may change when we open a new site. Thus they must be carefully followed. With this in mind, we define different spaces of integrable Hamiltonians and show embedding properties.

3.3.1.
Spaces

Definition 3.13

(Integrable Hamiltonians)
.

Let
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{Z}
be a finite set,
r\in(0,\frac{1}{4})
,
\mathcal{O}\subset((4r)^{2},1)^{\mathcal{S}}
,
\eta\geq 0
.
A Hamiltonian
P\in\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
is integrable if its non zero coefficients
P_{n,\boldsymbol{k},\boldsymbol{m}}^{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\neq 0
satisfy

(60)

\boldsymbol{k}=0\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\exists\varphi\in\mathfrak{S}_{\#\boldsymbol{\ell}},\ \varphi\boldsymbol{\ell}=\boldsymbol{\ell}\ \mathrm{and}\ \varphi\boldsymbol{\sigma}=-\boldsymbol{\sigma}

We denote by
\Pi_{\mathrm{int}}
the associated projection defined by restriction of the coefficients
17
17

17

i.e. for
K\in\mathscr{H}_{\eta,r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
,
I=\Pi_{\mathrm{int}}K
is the Hamiltonian whose coefficients are defined by
I_{n,\boldsymbol{k},\boldsymbol{m}}^{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}=\mathbbm{1}_{\eqref{eq:integ}}K_{n,\boldsymbol{k},\boldsymbol{m}}^{\boldsymbol{\ell},\boldsymbol{\sigma}}
.

Definition 3.14

(Quartic integrable Hamiltonians
\mathscr{Q}^{\mathrm{cste}}_{\mathcal{S}},\mathscr{Q}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
)
.

Let
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{Z}
be a finite set. We denote by
\mathscr{Q}^{\mathrm{cste}}_{\mathcal{S}}
, the set of the formal series
Q
, of the form

Q(\mu,y,u):=\sum_{k,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}Q_{k,\ell}Y_{k}Y_{\ell}+\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}Q_{k}Y_{k}\mu+Q_{\emptyset}\mu^{2}

where
Y_{k}:=\mathbbm{1}_{k\in\mathcal{S}}y_{k}+\mathbbm{1}_{k\in\mathcal{S}^{c}}|u_{k}|^{2}
and whose coefficients
Q_{k,\ell},Q_{k},Q_{\emptyset}\in\mathbb{R}
satisfy the symmetry condition
Q_{k,\ell}=Q_{\ell,k}
and the bound

\|Q\|_{\mathscr{Q}^{\mathrm{cste}}}:=\sup_{k,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}|Q_{k,\ell}|\Lambda_{k,\ell}^{-1}\vee|Q_{k}|\langle k\rangle^{\delta}\vee Q_{\emptyset}<\infty

where

(61)

\Lambda_{k,\ell}:=1\wedge\frac{\langle k\rangle^{4\delta}}{\langle\ell\rangle^{\delta}}\wedge\frac{\langle\ell\rangle^{4\delta}}{\langle k\rangle^{\delta}}.

Moreover, if
\mathcal{O}\subset\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\mathcal{S}}
, we set

\mathscr{Q}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}:=\mathrm{Lip}(\mathcal{O};\mathscr{Q}^{\mathrm{cste}}_{\mathcal{S}}).

Definition 3.15

(Linear Hamiltonians in the actions
\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cste}}_{\mathcal{S}},\mathscr{L}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
)
.

Let
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{Z}
be a finite set. We denote by
\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cste}}_{\mathcal{S}}
, the set of the formal series
L
, of the form

L(y):=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}L_{k}Y_{k}

where
Y_{k}:=\mathbbm{1}_{k\in\mathcal{S}}y_{k}+\mathbbm{1}_{k\in\mathcal{S}^{c}}|u_{k}|^{2}
and whose coefficients
L_{k}\in\mathbb{R}
satisfy the bound

\|L\|_{\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cste}}}:=\sup_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}|L_{k}|\langle k\rangle^{\delta}<\infty.

Moreover, if
\mathcal{O}\subset\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\mathcal{S}}
, we set

\mathscr{L}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}:=\mathrm{Lip}(\mathcal{O};\mathscr{L}^{\mathrm{cste}}_{\mathcal{S}}).

Definition 3.16

(Almost constant Hamiltonians
\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{cste}},\mathscr{A}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
)
.

We denote by
\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{cste}}
, the set of the affine functions
A
of the form

A(\mu):=A_{0}+A_{1}\mu

with
A_{0},A_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
,
that we equip with the norm

\|A\|_{\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{cste}}}:=3^{-1}|A_{0}|\vee|A_{1}|.

Moreover, if
\mathcal{O}\subset\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\mathcal{S}}
, where
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{Z}
is a finite set, we set

\mathscr{A}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}:=\mathrm{Lip}(\mathcal{O};\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{cste}}).

Remark 3.17
.

The coefficients
3
is convenient but of minor importance in this paper. It is only used to have a factor close to
1
in the estimate (
121
). We introduce the factor
3
in Definition
3.23
below for the same reason.

Definition 3.18

(Hamiltonians in normal form
\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
)
.

Let
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{Z}
be a finite set and
\mathcal{O}\subset\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\mathcal{S}}
.
An Hamiltonian is said in normal form if it belongs to the space
\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
defined by

(62)

\mathscr{N}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}:=\mathscr{A}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}\oplus\mathscr{L}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}\oplus\mathscr{Q}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}.

3.3.2.
Lipschitz norms

We are going to define some well suited norms on the Lipschitz spaces defined above.

Definition 3.19

(Norms for
\mathscr{A}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
)
.

Let
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{Z}
be a finite set,
\mathcal{O}\subset\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\mathcal{S}}
and
A\in\mathscr{A}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
. We set

\|A\|_{\mathscr{A}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}}:=\sup_{\xi\in\mathcal{O}}\|A(\xi)\|_{\mathscr{A}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{cste}}},\quad\|A\|_{\mathscr{A}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{lip}}}:=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi,\zeta\in\mathcal{O}\\
\xi\neq\zeta\end{subarray}}\frac{\|A(\xi)-A(\zeta)\|_{\mathscr{A}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{cste}}}}{\|\xi-\zeta\|_{\ell^{1}}},

\|A\|_{\mathscr{A}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}}:=\|A\|_{\mathscr{A}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}}+\|A\|_{\mathscr{A}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{lip}}}.

Definition 3.20

(Some norms for
\mathscr{L}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
)
.

Let
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{Z}
be a finite set,
\mathcal{O}\subset\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\mathcal{S}}
and
L\in\mathscr{L}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
. We set

\|L\|_{\mathscr{L}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}}:=\sup_{\xi\in\mathcal{O}}\|L(\xi)\|_{\mathscr{L}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{cste}}},\quad\|L\|_{\mathscr{L}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{lip}}}:=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi,\zeta\in\mathcal{O}\\
\xi\neq\zeta\end{subarray}}\frac{\|L(\xi)-L(\zeta)\|_{\mathscr{L}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{cste}}}}{\|\xi-\zeta\|_{\ell^{1}}},

\|L\|_{\mathscr{L}_{r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}}:=\|L\|_{\mathscr{L}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}}+r^{2}\|L\|_{\mathscr{L}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{lip}}}.

Definition 3.21

(Norms for
\mathscr{Q}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
)
.

Let
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{Z}
be a finite set,
\mathcal{O}\subset\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\mathcal{S}}
,
Q\in\mathscr{Q}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
and
r>0
. We set

\|Q\|_{\mathscr{Q}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}}:=\sup_{\xi\in\mathcal{O}}\|Q(\xi)\|_{\mathscr{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{cste}}},\quad\|Q\|_{\mathscr{Q}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{lip}}}:=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi,\zeta\in\mathcal{O}\\
\xi\neq\zeta\end{subarray}}\frac{\|Q(\xi)-Q(\zeta)\|_{\mathscr{Q}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{cste}}}}{\|\xi-\zeta\|_{\ell^{1}}},

\|Q\|_{\mathscr{Q}_{r,\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{tot}}}:=\|Q\|_{\mathscr{Q}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{sup}}}+r^{2}\|Q\|_{\mathscr{Q}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}^{\mathrm{lip}}}.

The elements of
\mathscr{L}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
are frequencies on which we need strong Lipschitz estimates to be able to prove small divisor estimates (see Lemma
4.1
). The most critical terms appear when we "recenter" an element of
\mathscr{Q}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
(see Proposition
7.2
and in particular equations (
123
) and (
125
)). Therefore, we now introduce a more subtle Lipschitz norm on
\mathscr{L}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
allowing to control these critical terms.

Definition 3.22

(Directional Lipschitz norms for
\mathscr{L}_{\mathcal{O},\mathcal{S}}
)
.

Let
\mathcal{S}\subset\mathbb{Z}
be a finite set,
